Observed 2026-09-30/10-01 in ses_f0b92a96fffeTf969H1IQU0pMs: DB tail shows two `assistant []` rows + one `step-start,reasoning,step-finish` row; auto_resume.log shows status=retry → session.error (00:41:16Z); the maintainer observed CUDA errors on the backend at the same window (trigger CONFIRMED 2026-10-01 by the maintainer: an over-aggressive GPU clock speed — stable in gaming but not in inference mode — caused the backend CUDA errors and the mid-step kills; not compaction). REVISED 2026-10-01 (maintainer observation): the backend is SELF-HEALING — after an error it restarts and the interrupted message is taken back up exactly where it left (re-prefill), continuing seamlessly (observed mid-sentence). The \"session halts until a manual user message\" symptom was most likely caused by the maintainer's MANUAL interruptions while observing the interruptions (colliding with the auto-retry cycle), not by a pure retry-path defect. Downgraded to SUSPECTED: confirm only by observing a future error WITHOUT manual interference. If real, the fix belongs in the fork's retry/derivation path (strip or merge trailing assistant rows before resending, or don't persist the row before the step succeeds). Evidence: DB message tail + auto_resume.log + maintainer backend observation + maintainer manual-interrupt admission.\n- **cured 2026-10-01 (planner-1, plan1 maintenance pass):** fork-domain finding — recorded in the backend-ops knowledge entry (`agent/knowledge/knowledge_plugins.md`, 2026-10-01: the SUSPECTED retry-path status + the no-manual-interrupt operational rule); the potential fix (strip/merge trailing assistant rows in the fork's retry/derivation path) belongs to the maintainer's FORK (his domain) — no agent TODO; confirm only by an un-interfered future observation.\n\n## 2026-10-01_01-59 planner_Q3S_slow ses_f0b92a96fffeTf969H1IQU0pMs\nopencode live host: in-process tool's spawned WRITE sqlite call 2500 ms-kills while the identical call from bash takes 19 ms — MECHANISM REVISED 2026-10-01 (same session, after the fork's DB layer was checked): NOT a lock/deadlock (the fork uses bun:sqlite via a static import that works in the compiled exe, semaphore-scoped short executes, no transaction spanning tool execution — the original deadlock candidate is withdrawn). The real cause is the SAME family as the TAB-separator collapse: Bun's Windows command-line join mangles double-quote-bearing argv — the context_trim tail SQL inlined a JSON.stringify literal (double quotes) → the CLI lost its SQL arg → interactive mode → stdin hang; the report's single-quote-only SQL works live, and bash (correct quoting) runs the identical write in 19 ms. FIXED in context_trim itself (json_set inside sqlite — the SQL text carries no double quotes; smoke 26/26) — pending live verification after the next restart.   [text]
**1. auto_resume — what the v1 plugin does, and what the CLI handler is**

v1 legs (from `.opencode/plugin/auto_resume.ts` header): UNIT 1 event log + surface probe; UNIT 2 the PASSIVE ctx-line nudge (already carried into the v2 `context_family` by unit 3b — nothing left to port there); UNIT 3 the spawn helper (`create()` + ONE queued prompt + the spawn marker/ident); UNIT 4 the restart / recovery-continue branch + the `action:` line; plus the #109 silent-limit-stop detector and the lineage/counter legs. The resume channel in v1 is in-process (`ctx.client.session.*`) — your read is right, no CLI involved today.

v2 has the same shape in-process, verified at fork `packages/plugin/src/promise/session.ts:155-176`: `SessionDomain` = `Pick<SessionApi, "create"|"get"|"remove"|"switchAgent"|"switchModel"|"prompt"|"generate"|"command"|"compact"|"synthetic"|"interrupt"|"update"|"move"|"wait"|"context">` + hooks. So order 2 ports 1:1 plugin-side: idle/status event → `session.compact` (SELF) or `session.create` + `session.prompt` (successor). One detail still unverified: `create`'s param shape (does it take agent + model?) — one check before I spec it.

The CLI handler I named = `opencode run --continue` / `--session <id>` (+ `--fork`, `--agent`, `--model`, `--standalone`) → `runNonInteractive` in a NEW process against the server (fork `packages/cli/src/commands/handlers/run.ts:11-31`). That is a process-level resume — spawn a fresh host process bound to the DB session, the analogue of your retired looprunner. Not needed for the plugin port; it only helps if you want the loop driven externally.

**2. context_recovery** — tabled per your ruling, recorded, no code, no TODO entry (commit `8937948`).

**3. Sync — the two trees are ONE repo, not two**

MAIN (`main`) and the clone (`migration`) share `origin` `wasiejen/opencode-agent`: merge-base `3297f1b`, MAIN ahead 67 commits, the clone's `origin/main` stale at `3bdfbd6`. So git does the sync; no copying, no manifest of "old" files:

- Feed MAIN into the clone: `git -C ../opencode-agent-v2 fetch ../opencode-agent main` (a path remote — no GitHub round-trip).
- Read-only dry run (`git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only main migration`) reported EXACTLY 6 conflicts: the probe, the two v1 block_transfer smokes, `.opencode/tools/block_transfer.ts` — those three ARE the #143 stale twins — plus `agent/handover/handover_task_to_planner.md` and `opencode.jsonc`. Everything else auto-merges.
- Boundary rule at merge time: take MAIN for the v1 estate (`git checkout --theirs -- .opencode/tools .opencode/plugin/probes .opencode/plugin/tests`) — this closes #143 automatically (bt pins go 132→151 etc.) — and take the branch for the branch-local state (`git checkout --ours -- opencode.jsonc agent/handover loop/`). The v2 estate (`.opencode/plugins/`) exists only on `migration`, so a merge never touches it.

Answers:

**1. Does a merge transfer the v1 plugins back?** No.
- The `migration` branch already CONTAINS the full v1 snapshot (branched at 3297f1b) — that's why `.opencode/tools/block_transfer.ts` and the v1 smokes exist there at all. A merge applies only MAIN's changes SINCE the branch point; unchanged v1 files are untouched.
- The unported v1 plugins were MOVED, not deleted — commit `5516a83 move unported v1 plugins out of the v2 plugin discovery`, recorded as `R100` renames. Git carries MAIN's later edits to those files onto the NEW path (`plugin-unported-v1/…`). Nothing lands back at `.opencode/plugin/*.ts`.
- Measured (read-only) against MAIN's current main: 27 files touched, 7 conflicts: the probe, three v1 smokes (block_transfer, block_transfer.sandbox, gauge_core), `.opencode/tools/block_transfer.ts`, `agent/handover/handover_task_to_planner.md`, `opencode.jsonc`. Everything else (TODO, NAP, knowledge, docs) auto-merges.
- The trap I'd guard: if main ADDS a NEW plugin file under `.opencode/plugin/`, that path IS a v2 discovery root (`PluginSourceDirectory.names = ["plugin","plugins"]`, fork `packages/core/src/plugin/source-directory.ts:7`) → the v2 host would try to load a v1-shaped plugin. One post-merge sweep fixes it.
- Your model "everything from main kept except what's under `.opencode/*`" is close but inverted for the v1 estate: main's v1-side edits are exactly what you WANT to keep in the branch (in the inert copies); what you must protect is only the branch-local stuff: `opencode.jsonc`, `agent/handover/**`, `loop/**`.

**Re: "why take MAIN's v1 twins if they're not adapted to v2?"** Because in that tree they're not candidates-to-adapt, they're the FIXTURES the clone gate runs: the clone's own gate executes the 12 v1 node smokes (bt 132/132, sandbox 76/76) + the probe (repointed at `plugin-unported-v1` in #141). Consequence of staleness is proven, not hypothetical: #143 — the 5a worker read the clone's `submit.ts` (167 lines) and the 5c spec had to be re-baselined onto MAIN's 1034-line twin mid-wave. Refreshing = the v2 branch's regression baseline stays honest + the next port anchors on the right behavior. The adaptation itself stays a separate act (the `.opencode/plugins/*.ts` files) — a merge NEVER touches those, they exist only on `migration`. If you'd rather carry no v1 estate in the v2 branch, that's coherent, but then the branch loses those pins and the probe needs cross-tree imports (the #141 shim precedent) — my recommendation: keep + refresh.

**2.** Ruled plugin-driven — recorded (commit 781a3ea). Remaining check before I spec it: does `session.create` take agent + model?

**3.** Yes — the script's whole point is preview-before-execute: `--dry-run` prints the merge-tree conflict set + the resolution plan with zero mutation; `--apply` does fetch → merge --no-commit → the two resolution glob sets → the guard sweep → the clone gate, and ABORTS the merge if any suite is red. Questions (≤3):

3. When to switch the host to v2: now (so the pending v2 live-acceptance sweep lands) or after auto_resume v2 lands (a v2 host without the loop plugin = manual restarts)? Recommend: after auto_resume lands; but the sweep is valuable evidence, so a short trial run of the v2 host is worth it — your call.

He's pushing back on my "branch-local `opencode.jsonc`, `agent/handover/**`, `loop/**`" recommendation. His point: MAIN's copies of the handover/loop files and opencode.jsonc are the up-to-date ones — so why keep the outdated branch-local version?

He's right in substance, and I should say so plainly (pushback over accommodation: state disagreement before refinement — here I DISAGREE with my own earlier recommendation after his argument; state it).

Let me think carefully about the actual mechanics, because there's a real subtlety:

Two working trees, two branches, ONE repo. Currently:
- MAIN tree = `main` branch = where the live host runs (v1) and where all the planner/worker bookkeeping happens (NAP, loop_log, handover, counter). It's ahead 67.
- The clone tree = `migration` branch = where the v2 estate lives and where the v2-era workers ran (the #137 units were run by workers in the CLONE tree — they committed to `migration`, wrote loop_log DONE lines... to which tree? The v2 plugin loop_log's repo root = setup-captured ctx.location.directory → the clone's own loop folder; the 5c worker's "DONE line in loop_log" was recorded in the CLONE (the plan21 note says "worker DONE line in loop_log" — and the clone's log shows migration-side commits like b8592d5 with NAP entries).

Also `opencode.jsonc` at the clone root = the v2 host's config (the standalone v2.0.29 in the clone root — the v2 host's own config: plugins discovery, agents, permissions). It is NOT simply "outdated": it's a different host's config (v2 schema — different keys/shapes; e.g. v2 config differs: `permission.external_directory` array shape vs v1 object; the 5c port handled BOTH config shapes!). MAIN's opencode.jsonc is v1-shaped and the v1 host's; if you take MAIN's file into the migration branch, the v2 host would read a config whose provider/model entries point at v1 model ids and permission shapes.

So the honest answer splits:
1. `agent/handover/**` + `loop/**`: he's right — MAIN is the sole writer of plan state today; the migration-side NAP entries exist because v2 workers ran in that tree. If he decides ONE plan of record (MAIN's), the right rule is: take MAIN's version (`--theirs`) for these paths and stop the v2-side sessions from writing their own NAP/loop files... but the v2 host's plugins (loop_log, auto_resume v2, state_snapshot) inherently write to their own tree's loop/agent/handover paths when they run. Options:
   a. Share them (take MAIN's) → the v2 host's writes then conflict/churn with MAIN's next merge; and while the v2 host is running, ITS plan state must live somewhere.
   b. Point the v2 estate's paths at the MAIN tree (cross-tree writes — the v2 tools' root = setup-captured ctx.location.directory, which would be the clone root; you can't easily redirect without config).
   c. Share by MERGE and accept two-way churn (auto-merge usually fine because they append in different regions; the measured dry run showed NO conflict on handover_planner.md/loop_log.md — they auto-merged). So "take theirs" isn't even needed for most: they auto-merge; only handover_task_to_planner.md conflicted (because both branches wrote the same slot).
   So the practical rule for plan-state files: DON'T pin them `--ours`; let them merge, and resolve the handover_task*/counter by taking MAIN's side when the v2 tree isn't actively running a loop (i.e. mostly MAIN), since a stale handover copy is worse than a merged one.

2. `opencode.jsonc`: this one genuinely must stay branch-specific while BOTH hosts run, because it configures DIFFERENT hosts with different schemas (the v2 host at the clone root needs v2-shaped config: the 5c plugin reads the v2 `permissions:[{action,resource,effect}]` array shape; MAIN's v1 host reads the object shape). "Outdated" isn't the right frame — it's a different host. Once he switches the live host to v2 (his #3 plan: switch after auto_resume lands), the source of truth for the config flips to the migration branch's file — and then he'd want to copy/translate MAIN's config edits into the migration one manually (a raw merge of the JSONC would be wrong: providers/models/limits differ). So: for the config, recommend a rule = never merge (keep branch's own), plus a doc note listing the config keys that must be ported by hand (agents/models/permissions/limit.context).

Also, since he says MAIN's files contain the up-to-date info: he'd like ONE NAP/loop for both. That's coherent if the v2 host, when it runs, ALSO writes into the same paths — which it does (its own tree). Two trees → two copies. UNLESS he moves the v2 host to run from the MAIN tree (i.e., switch the MAIN tree's checkout to the migration branch — one tree!). That's the clean endgame: a single working tree that checks out `migration` (which contains everything: v2 estate + v1 inert estate + shared docs). Then there's exactly ONE opencode.jsonc, ONE NAP, ONE loop folder, and the "sync" problem reduces to a normal branch merge. That IS the answer to his #3 instinct too (switch the host to v2 = flip that tree's checkout), and then MAIN-branch churn merges INTO migration in the same tree — no dual-branch bookkeeping at all.

Wait — but does the v2 host run from the clone because it has a standalone v2.0.29 install in its root ("standalone opencode v2.0.29 in its root")? The knowledge said: the clone repo has "standalone opencode v2.0.29 in its root" — hmm, that might mean the clone tree contains a `node_modules`/`opencode` install used to run the v2 host. So the v2 host runs from the clone tree because of the install, not because of the branch. If he switches the MAIN tree to the `migration` branch, he'd need the v2 binary there (the installed opencode version is per-process/global?). The installed opencode version = 1.18.32 for MAIN (v1). The v2 host = the standalone v2.0.29 in the clone root. So running v2 in the MAIN tree = moving the standalone install (his domain, and plausible later).

So my answer should be:
- Agree with his correction: for the plan-state paths, MAIN is the source of truth → the rule should be "take MAIN's side" (not `--ours`), and the measured merge already auto-merges everything except `handover_task_to_planner.md` (which is a scratch channel — MAIN's side wins by default, both copies' content is throwaway).
- Correct my `opencode.jsonc` claim: it is not outdated, it is a DIFFERENT HOST's config (v2 schema: array permission entries, v2 agent ids/models; the 5c plugin's dual-shape handling exists because of this). Answer content:

**1. On his pushback — he's right about the plan state; my rule was wrong there, corrected and now implemented in the script.**
- `agent/handover/**` + `loop/**`: I now take MAIN's side (`--theirs`) — as he says, MAIN is the only writer today, and MAIN's copies are the live ones. Measured: those paths auto-merge anyway (only `handover_task_to_planner.md` conflicted).
- `opencode.jsonc`: this one is NOT "outdated" — it is a different host's config. The clone root holds the standalone v2 install; its config uses the v2 shapes (`permissions: [{action, resource, effect}]` array entries + the v2 agent/model rows), while MAIN's file is v1-shaped and drives the v1 fork.
```js
  // Rule (A) (maintainer ruling 2026-10-23): MAIN's probe lands, then the branch
  // patch repoints its v1 plugin imports into `.opencode/plugin-unported-v1/`.
  const unported = new Set(fs.readdirSync(path.join(o.clone, UNPORTED_DIR)).filter(f => f.endsWith(".ts")));
  const pr = path.join(o.clone, ...BRANCH_ADAPTED[0].split("/"));
  if (fs.existsSync(pr)) {
    const src = fs.readFileSync(pr, "utf8");
    const out = src.replace(/\.opencode\/plugin\/([A-Za-z0-9_.\-\/]+\.ts)/g, m => {
      const b = m.split("/").pop();
      return unported.has(b) ? m.replace("/plugin/", "/plugin-unported-v1/") : m;
    });
    if (out !== src) { fs.writeFileSync(pr, out); console.log("patch: probe v1-plugin imports repointed (" + (src.match(/\.opencode\/plugin\/[A-Za-z0-9_.\-\/]+\.ts/g) || []).length + " sites)"); }
  }
  const left = gitAt(o.clone, ["diff", "--name-only", "--diff-filter=U"]).stdout.split("\n").filter(x => x.trim());
  if (left.length) { for (const p of left) console.log("unmerged-left: " + p); console.log("state: merge aborted, nothing applied"); execPrint("git", ["-C", o.clone, "merge", "--abort"]); process.exit(1); }
```
